- **Where:** `tab-manager.js:820` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Tabs A, B and C are open in group 1, and tab A has a per-tab proxy (set-tab-proxy). After tab C is opened, tab A's requests carry 'tab_C_…', so _resolveUpstreamForTab uses tab C's config (the global proxy) and tab A's traffic leaves without its assigned proxy. MITM logs and gateway-error banners are attributed to tab C.
- **Fix:** In applyCupNetRidHeaderToSession, drop the tabId closure and install the listener only once per session, for example guarded by a WeakSet of sessions. Inside the callback, resolve the tab id per request with `const tid = getTabIdByWebContentsId(details.webContentsId) || 'unknown';` (the function already exists at tab-manager.js:1099). createTab and setTabCookieGroup can keep calling it, because the WeakSet makes repeat calls no-ops. If the unknown case matters, fall back to the last tab created on that session.
- _Reported 4× (also: azuretls-fingerprint, tabs-shell-main)_

#### A-064 🟡 Breakpoint 'Forward' and 'Apply & forward': header edits are ignored and binary bodies are replaced with UTF-8 text
- **Where:** `breakpoint-renderer.js:53` · **Category:** ux-broken · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The user pauses a gzip or multipart upload and clicks plain 'Forward'. The upload arrives corrupted. The user edits the User-Agent in the headers JSON and clicks 'Apply & forward'. The original User-Agent is still sent.
- **Fix:** breakpoint-renderer.js: make the bp-forward button call `api.resume({id, action:'forward', patch:null})`. In doForward for 'Apply & forward', compare against the loaded snapshot and include only the fields that changed. Include body only if the textarea differs from its initial value; otherwise keep bodyBase64. In applyBreakpointPatch, when patch.headers is given without patch.orderedHeaders, set opts.headers = {...patch.headers} (replace, not merge). Then rebuild opts.orderedHeaders from the existing order: update the values of kept keys, drop deleted keys and append new ones, reusing the same reconcile helper proposed for finding 2.

#### A-065 🟡 Single breakpoint window: concurrent breakpoints overwrite each other and earlier ones cannot be resumed
- **Where:** `breakpoint-renderer.js:30` · **Category:** ux-broken · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A breakpoint rule on 'https://api.x.com/*' matches three parallel XHRs. The window shows only the third. The first two hang for 30 seconds and then go out unmodified, even though the user intended to block them.
- **Fix:** In breakpoint-renderer.js, replace `currentId` with a Map or queue of pending payloads and render it as a list the user can select from. After api.resume returns ok, remove that id and show the next one, or clear the form. In breakpoint-service.js, export a function that returns the pending ids (or resumeAll(action)). In sub-windows.js, have d.breakpointWindow.on('closed') call breakpointService.resumeBreakpoint(id, {action:'forward'}) for every pending id.

#### A-151 🟡 Leaf certificates do not chain to the CA: issuer DN mismatch and malformed AuthorityKeyIdentifier
- **Where:** `mitm-ca.js:74` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The exported CA (mitm-get-ca-cert) is installed in the OS or in curl for ExternalProxyPort clients. Every HTTPS request through the external port fails certificate validation.
- **Fix:** In mitm-ca.js buildCert, accept an issuer DN parameter. For leaves, pass the CA subject exactly: SEQ([encodeRDN('550403','CupNet MITM CA'), encodeRDN('55040a','CupNet'), encodeRDN('550406','US')]). Build it once as a shared constant (CA_SUBJECT) used for both the CA subject and the leaf issuer. Change the AKI at L90-91 to `OCT(SEQ([asn1(0x80, authorityKeyIdBytes)]))`. Optional hardening: add EKU serverAuth (OID 2.5.29.37 → 1.3.6.1.5.5.7.3.1) on leaves and keyUsage keyCertSign|cRLSign (critical) on the CA. Existing persisted CAs (loadOrGenerateCA) keep their subject, so the fix does not require regenerating them as long as the issuer DN matches byte for byte.

#### A-152 🟡 WebSocket tunnel with a DNS override uses the IP as the SNI and cert hostname, so wss always fails
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:1759` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A DNS rule maps api.example.com to 10.0.0.5. HTTPS requests work because they go through AzureTLS with the Host header, but new WebSocket('wss://api.example.com/ws') always gets 502 'Hostname/IP does not match certificate's altnames'.
- **Fix:** At L1759 use const logicalHost = up.dnsOverride?.host || uObj.hostname; (optionally up.dnsOverride?.rewriteHost || up.dnsOverride?.host if a rewrite host should drive the SNI). Keep connectHost = up.dnsOverride?.ip || logicalHost. When logging, use the pre-override URL (mitmOpts.interceptMatchUrl || url) instead of up.url.

#### A-153 🟡 Native fallback returns compressed bodies but buildHttpResponse strips Content-Encoding, so the page receives garbage
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:43` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The worker hits ECONNRESET on a GET for an HTML page. The native fallback succeeds, and the tab renders binary garbage or a broken script or stylesheet.
- **Fix:** In _nativeHttpsFallback, delete every case variant of accept-encoding from finalHeaders, or decompress in res.on('end') according to res.headers['content-encoding'] (zlib.gunzipSync / inflateSync / brotliDecompressSync / zstd if available) before base64 encoding, then delete content-encoding. Better still, do not use the fallback when a proxy is configured or egress is blocked, and use rejectUnauthorized: true.

#### A-154 🟡 HTTP/1.1 auto-retry replays non-idempotent requests (POST/PUT/DELETE)
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:1473` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A payment or 'create order' POST goes through an Envoy-fronted origin. Envoy returns 503 with 'upstream connect error … reset reason: connection termination' after the backend already processed the request. The MITM re-sends the POST over HTTP/1.1 and the order is created twice.
- **Fix:** In mitm-proxy.js, add `isIdempotent &&` to both conditions: line 1473 `if (!forceHttp1 && isIdempotent && _shouldAutoRetryHttp1(e.message))` and line 1480 `if (!forceHttp1 && isIdempotent && _responseSuggestsHttp1Retry(res))`. The isIdempotent declaration (currently line 1432) must come before these lines, and it already does. Optionally, allow non-idempotent methods only for errors that prove nothing was sent, such as h2 REFUSED_STREAM or a connect failure.

#### A-155 🟡 WebSocket upgrade removes all 'error' listeners from the client TLS socket, so an RST causes an uncaught ECONNRESET and leaks the upstream socket
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:1221` · **Category:** error-handling · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A user navigates away while a wss handshake is waiting on a slow upstream. A 'critical uncaughtException ECONNRESET' is logged each time, and the upstream TLS socket stays open until the server closes it.
- **Fix:** Replace the removeAllListeners calls with removeListener for the specific named 'data' and 'close' handlers. Leave 'error' in place, or add `clientTls.on('error', () => {})` as the first line of _tunnelWebSocketUpgradeMitm. In _continueWsTunnelAfterPlan, check `if (clientTls.destroyed) { upSock.destroy(); return; }` before writing or piping. Also add `clientTls.once('close', () => { try { upSock.destroy(); } catch {} })` as soon as upSock is obtained.
- _Reported 2× (also: same area)_

#### A-158 🟡 ExternalProxyPort listens only on 127.0.0.1, but the UI advertises a LAN endpoint
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:2190` · **Category:** ux-broken · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The user starts an external proxy port and copies the 'LAN' curl command to a phone or another machine. It never connects.
- **Fix:** Choose one of two fixes. (a) Bind '0.0.0.0' at mitm-proxy.js:2190 and update the log line; Basic auth is already enforced by _checkAuth in _handleConnect/_handleHttp. Consider refusing to start with an empty password when binding to all interfaces. (b) Keep loopback as a deliberate security choice: fix the class comment at L2161 and have new-tab.html showOn stop populating epEndpoints.lan and the LAN curl command (hasLan=false).

#### A-160 🟡 Fully buffered request/response model breaks SSE/streaming, downloads over 30s and uploads over 10MB
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:1186` · **Category:** ux-broken · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** EventSource or streaming fetch (for example LLM chat UIs) shows nothing until the stream ends and then fails at 30s. A 200MB download over a slow link fails with a 502 timeout. Uploading a 50MB video resets the connection.
- **Fix:** Minimal: (a) when the parsed Content-Length exceeds the cap, write 'HTTP/1.1 413 Payload Too Large\r\nContent-Length: 0\r\nConnection: close\r\n\r\n' before ending the socket instead of calling destroy(), and read the cap from networkPolicy (env CUPNET_MITM_MAX_REQUEST_BODY). (b) Raise workerRequestMs/upstreamRequestMs defaults or make them per-request for large downloads. Full fix: add chunked NDJSON streaming frames (head frame, then body chunks) to the Go worker and to _doRequest/buildHttpResponse, at minimum for text/event-stream and bodies above a threshold.

#### A-178 🟡 Script rules: header edits and deletions on ctx.headers are not applied to the wire request
- **Where:** `request-interceptor.js:458` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A user writes a script rule to replace Authorization or remove Cookie. The rule shows as matched in the activity log and in ctx.headers, but the upstream still receives the original header.
- **Fix:** Rewrite mergeHeadersIntoOrdered to reconcile the two lists. Build a lowercase map from headers. For each ordered pair, drop it if its lowercase key is absent from the map, otherwise replace its value with the map's value, then mark the key as seen. Append the remaining keys at the end. One exception: when the script changed orderedHeaders itself (compare against a snapshot taken before the script ran), treat orderedHeaders as authoritative and rebuild headers from it.

#### A-179 🟡 detachFromSession strips the X-CupNet-Rid injector from the shared session while other tabs still use it
- **Where:** `request-interceptor.js:986` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — _removeAllHooks no longer clears shared webRequest hooks.
- **Impact:** Tab 1 is attached (refcount 1). The user opens tab 2 with the '+' button (no attach) and closes tab 1. The refcount drops to 0 and the rid injector is removed from persist group 1. Tab 2's requests then have no X-CupNet-Rid: tabId is null, per-tab upstream is ignored, and MITM/CDP dedup falls back to the time window, so duplicate log rows appear.
- **Fix:** In request-interceptor.js _removeAllHooks, delete the two lines `tabSession.webRequest.onBeforeSendHeaders(null)` and `tabSession.webRequest.onHeadersReceived(null)` and keep only the protocol.unhandle calls. Since the interceptor installs no webRequest hooks, the refcount no longer controls anything destructive. Optionally also make the new-tab and open-settings-tab IPCs call attachToSession to keep the refcount symmetric.

#### A-323 ⚪ CONNECT to non-TLS ports (ws://, http over CONNECT) always fails because the MITM always starts a TLS server
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:1075` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** new WebSocket('ws://localhost-dev-server.example:8080/socket') or any ws:// endpoint fails immediately in every tab. Dev tooling and HMR over ws:// break.
- **Fix:** In _handleConnect, after writing the 200, wait for the first chunk with socket.once('readable'), or read it and then unshift it back. If chunk[0] === 0x16, continue with the TLSSocket path. Otherwise, socket.unshift(chunk) and route it to a plaintext handler: parse the request, and if _isWebSocketUpgrade, tunnel to hostname:port with net.connect (and no TLS). Otherwise use the _handleHttp path, with the URL built as http://hostname:port.
- _Reported 2× (also: same area)_

#### A-324 ⚪ Worker spawn failure (EACCES/ENOENT) never restarts and waitReady hangs 120s with a misleading error
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:416` · **Category:** error-handling · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The packaged Go binary loses its +x bit or is quarantined (EACCES), or is replaced during an update while the app runs. The MITM start hangs for 2 minutes and then shows a wrong hint, and a mid-session crash-restart leaves the worker permanently dead.
- **Fix:** In `proc.on('error')`, when `this.proc` is this child and it never started (`!proc.pid`, or no 'spawn' event yet): set `this.ready = false`, run `this.emit('error', err)` so waitReady rejects at once (the emit is safe because waitReady attaches a listener; otherwise guard with `this.listenerCount('error')`), and schedule the same backoff restart as the exit handler. The simplest version: move the restart and cleanup body from 'exit' into a shared function, then call it from 'close' (which fires in both cases) or from both 'exit' and a spawn-failure 'error', with a once-guard. Also make the waitReady timeout message depend on `this._workerType`.
- _Reported 2× (also: same area)_

#### A-325 ⚪ stdin backpressure is defeated: _drainStdinQueue clears the draining flag in finally while waiting for 'drain'
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:526` · **Category:** perf · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A burst of large uploads fills the pipe. The queue never holds anything, so workerStdinQueueMax never triggers, memory grows in the stream buffer, and MaxListenersExceededWarning appears for 'drain'.
- **Fix:** Track a waiting state: `let waiting = false;` then in the `!ok` branch set `waiting = true;` before `this.proc.stdin.once('drain', () => { this._stdinDraining = false; this._drainStdinQueue(); }); return;`. Change the `finally` to `if (!waiting) finish();`.

#### A-326 ⚪ ExternalProxyPort plain-HTTP path uses only the first TCP chunk (max 8 KB) as the request
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:2223` · **Category:** data-loss · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A curl -x ext-port POST of a 50 KB JSON body to an http:// URL: the upstream receives a truncated body, since Content-Length is stripped and recomputed from the partial string.
- **Fix:** In ExternalProxyPort._handleConnection, for non-CONNECT heads, accumulate chunks the same way MitmProxy._readFullHttpRequestThenHandle does: wait for \r\n\r\n, parse Content-Length, wait for bodyStart+CL bytes, and enforce a 10 MB cap. Then call this._handleHttp(socket, buf.toString('utf8', 0, end)). The simplest way is to extract the accumulator into a shared module-level helper `readFullHttpRequest(socket, firstChunk, cb)` and use it from both classes. For the initial CONNECT detection, check the first chunk only.

#### A-327 ⚪ HEAD responses are rewritten to 'Content-Length: 0'
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:2147` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A download manager or video player probes size with fetch(url, {method:'HEAD'}) and reads Content-Length 0, so progress bars and range planning break.
- **Fix:** Add a third argument, reqMethod, to buildHttpResponse. When /^HEAD$/i.test(reqMethod) and the upstream headers include content-length (case-insensitive), write that value instead of bodyBuf.length. Keep dropping it for all other responses. Pass req.method / up.method at the call sites that serve upstream responses.

#### A-328 ⚪ Egress-blocked 502s treat every sub-resource as a document navigation and spam the gateway-error banner
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:1402` · **Category:** ux-broken · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** With egress blocked, loading one page sends dozens of page-gateway-error events to the shell.
- **Fix:** In _doRequest's egress-blocked branch, pass a request object that has a path, e.g. `let p = '/'; try { p = new URL(url).pathname; } catch {}` then `req: { method: opts.method, headers: opts.headers, path: p }`. As an alternative, let isLikelyDocumentNavigation derive the path from req.url when req.path is missing.

#### A-329 ⚪ Plain-HTTP path forwards the 'Proxy-Connection' hop-by-hop header to origin servers
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:1337` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** An http:// site behind anti-bot logic flags every CupNet request as proxied because of the Proxy-Connection header.
- **Fix:** In _handleHttp (and in the ExternalProxyPort plain-HTTP handler), add a hop-by-hop set, e.g. const HOP = new Set(['proxy-authorization','proxy-connection','keep-alive','te','trailer','upgrade']). Delete those keys from `headers` and exclude them in the orderedHeaders filter: `if (HOP.has(kl)) return false;`. Keep 'connection' (Go handles it) and exclude 'upgrade' only when the request is not a WebSocket upgrade.

#### A-361 ⚪ matchesPattern does not escape '?', so glob URL patterns with a query string never match
- **Where:** `request-interceptor.js:960` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — matchesPattern escapes "?".
- **Impact:** A mock or block rule copied from DevTools with a wildcard in the query never fires, and the UI's test-url-match reports no match.
- **Fix:** In matchesPattern change the escape class to /[.+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g.

#### A-362 ⚪ A script rule discards response-header edits from earlier non-stop modifyHeaders rules
- **Where:** `request-interceptor.js:833` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Rule 1 (priority 10) adds response header 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin' and does not stop. Rule 2 (priority 5) is a logging script. The ACAO header is never added.
- **Fix:** In the script branch, build posts = [mergedModifyPost, scriptAfterObj].filter(Boolean). Return postProcess as that array (or null if empty) only when stopOnMatch(rule) is true. Otherwise store it and continue the loop. In finalizeMitmInterceptResponseAsync, accept an array and apply each entry in order. Keep single-object handling for backward compatibility.

#### A-363 ⚪ Mock rules never increment the rule hit counter
- **Where:** `request-interceptor.js:685` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — mock branch bumps the hit counter.
- **Impact:** The Rules UI shows 0 hits for a mock rule that is actively serving responses, so users think it is not matching.
- **Fix:** Add `_bumpRuleHit(rule);` at the start of the `if (rule.type === 'mock') {` branch (or just before its return at ~737).

### AzureTLS fingerprint worker

#### A-011 🟠 Pool slot released without waking waiters; a later __clear_sessions__ then deadlocks the Go worker for good
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/pool.go:175` · **Category:** race · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A proxy profile uses an unsupported scheme such as socks4:// (azuretls SetProxy fails with 'unsupported proxy scheme'), or the upstream resets connections (EOF / connection reset). A page load sends more than 4 concurrent requests on one tab key. All slot holders fail, and requests 5..N wait forever. The next setBrowser/setDnsOverrides sends __clear_sessions__, the Go loop blocks in waitZeroInflight forever, and every later MITM request times out after 30s. The process never exits, so AzureTLSWorker never restarts it.
- **Fix:** Add a helper in pool.go: `func releaseSlot(key string){ poolsMu.Lock(); p:=pools[key]; if p==nil {poolsMu.Unlock(); return}; p.inUse--; if len(p.waiters)>0 { ch:=p.waiters[0]; p.waiters=p.waiters[1:]; poolsMu.Unlock(); ch <- "retry"; return }; poolsMu.Unlock() }`. The waiter then re-loops, because unknown values fall through the for loop, and re-claims the slot through the inUse<max branch. Use releaseSlot in the borrowSession factory-error branch and in handleHTTPRequest's conn-error branch in place of the manual inUse--. Also give waitZeroInflight a deadline, e.g. 5-10s, after which closeAllPoolClients runs anyway and aborts the waiters.

#### A-012 🟠 Go worker merges multiple Set-Cookie headers into one line joined with ", ", so cookies are lost
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/request.go:58` · **Category:** data-loss · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A login response sets `sid=...; HttpOnly` and `csrf=...; Path=/`. Chromium parses one cookie named sid whose attributes contain garbage (the Path becomes "/, csrf=..."), and csrf is never stored. The login breaks on sites that set 2 or more cookies in one response, which is most of them. The log viewer's getSetCookies also mis-parses the line.
- **Fix:** In azuretls-go/request.go change outgoingLine.Headers to map[string]any and have headersToMap return map[string]any. For len(vals)>1, emit []string(vals) at least when http.CanonicalHeaderKey(k)=="Set-Cookie" (other headers can stay joined). Update the literal `Headers: map[string]string{}` sites in handleHTTPRequest and TestHeadersToMap to match. buildHttpResponse already handles array values.
- _Reported 3× (also: mitm-intercept, tool-windows-a)_

#### A-014 🟠 azuretls Session CookieJar is active: the worker stores upstream cookies and re-injects them into Chromium's Cookie header
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/request.go:142` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** (1) A user clears cookies or logs out through the cookie manager. Chromium sends no Cookie, but the worker still appends `sid=old`, so the user stays logged in or keeps being tracked. (2) JS updates document.cookie a=2, and the wire carries `a=2; a=1`. (3) Request Editor and IP-geo share the tab:__shared__ pool, so cookies from one replay leak into unrelated requests. (4) Requests round-robin across 4 sessions with different jars, so the cookie state differs between requests.
- **Fix:** In buildAzureRequest set `req.NoCookie = true` right after constructing req, before the `if bare { return }` so the bare path is covered too. For the legacy path, also send `no_cookie: true` from azure-tls-worker.js / azuretls/azureTLS.js.

#### A-059 🟠 setUserAgent(ua, '') clears the partition's Accept-Language whenever no language is configured
- **Where:** `user-agent-utils.js:102` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — only pass acceptLanguage to setUserAgent when configured; empty string was clearing Accept-Language (user-agent-utils.js).
- **Impact:** A default install with no profile language: tab requests reach MITM with no Accept-Language header while navigator.languages says en-US. That is a strong bot signal on Cloudflare/Akamai, and sites also fall back to the default locale.
- **Fix:** In applyRendererUserAgentToSession, add: `const lang = String(acceptLanguage || '').trim(); if (lang) sess.setUserAgent(ua, lang); else sess.setUserAgent(ua);`. Calling with the UA only keeps the partition's existing/default Accept-Language.

#### A-061 🟡 Lost wakeup and check-then-act race in waitIfClearing / httpInflight
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/main.go:22` · **Category:** race · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A burst of requests arrives right as a DNS-override clear finishes. One request hangs until the next clear (possibly forever), and MITM reports a 30s worker timeout for it.
- **Fix:** Replace the atomics with `var clearRW sync.RWMutex`. Request goroutines do `clearRW.RLock(); defer clearRW.RUnlock()` around unmarshal + handleHTTPRequest. The clear, run off the read loop per finding 1, does `clearRW.Lock(); closeAllPoolClients(); clearRW.Unlock()`. Minimal alternative: in waitIfClearing, take clearWaitersMu, re-check clearing.Load() and return if it is false, then append. Also do httpInflight.Add(1) first and re-check clearing afterwards (if set, Add(-1) and wait again).

#### A-062 🟡 Firefox/Safari/iOS HTTP/2 fingerprints are wrong, and ApplyHTTP2 forces Chrome HEADERS priority on every profile
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/profiles.go:27` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** ✅ PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-09-23 — corrected Firefox/Safari/iOS HTTP/2 strings in profiles.go + azure-tls-worker.js as part of the browser-template refresh. The separate concern (ApplyHTTP2 overriding azuretls presets) is still open.
- **Impact:** A profile with TLS template 'firefox' sends a Firefox ClientHello with a non-Firefox Akamai H2 fingerprint and Chrome-style HEADERS priority. Anti-bot systems such as Akamai and Cloudflare flag the mismatch, which is worse than using no template.
- **Fix:** In createPooledSession, call ApplyHTTP2 only when sessionBrowserName(prof) is chrome, edge or opera. For firefox, safari and ios, skip it so session.go:118 initTransport(s.Browser) -> initHTTP2 applies azuretls' per-browser settings, window update, header priority and pseudo-header order. Also set BrowserProfile.HTTP2 for those entries to the azuretls-equivalent strings, since they are only used for __get_profiles__ display, e.g. firefox '1:65536;2:0;4:131072;5:16384|12517377|0|m,p,a,s'. Update azure-tls-worker.js BROWSER_PROFILES and the TLS_TEMPLATE_DESCS text to match.
- _Reported 3× (also: same area)_

#### A-063 🟡 The bare fallback retries without cookies, auth or range headers and returns the result as the real response
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/request.go:294` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** An authenticated GET fails twice with EOF. The bare request returns a 302 to /login with `Set-Cookie: session=<new anonymous>`, Chromium overwrites the user's session cookie, and the user is logged out. A media Range request gets a 200 full body instead of a 206, which corrupts playback.
- **Fix:** Delete the bare fallback block (request.go:294-311) and return the fresh-retry error, e.g. `return outgoingLine{ID: id, StatusCode: 0, Headers: map[string]string{}, Error: err.Error()}`. Do the same in azure-tls-worker.js: remove Step B and send the retry error.
- _Reported 2× (also: mitm-intercept)_

#### A-107 🟡 Request Editor worker path ignores the fail-closed egress block (direct egress with the real IP)
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/log-compare-execute-ipc.js:411` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The proxy profile fails to connect and the UI shows 'egress blocked'. The user replays a captured request from the Request Editor, and it goes out directly from the real IP with the user's cookies and auth headers.
- **Fix:** At the top of the worker branch, add: `if (ctx.mitmProxy._egressBlocked && !currentProxy) { const out = { success:false, error:`Egress blocked: ${ctx.mitmProxy._egressBlockReason || 'Proxy required but unavailable'}`, duration:0 }; maybeLog(out); cleanupExec(); return { ...out, cancelToken }; }`. Apply the same guard before the net.fetch fallback. Also pass `ja3: ctx.mitmProxy._activeJa3 || undefined` and `orderedHeaders: Object.entries(sanitizedHeaders)` so the editor's header order is kept. Better still, route the call through a small mitmProxy helper that reuses _resolveUpstreamForTab(null).
- _Reported 2× (also: x-ipc-contract)_

#### A-161 🟡 A per-tab proxy profile inherits the global custom JA3 even when the tab profile uses a template
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:890` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The global profile uses custom Chrome JA3. Tab X gets a per-tab profile with the 'firefox' template. Tab X's requests use the firefox pool key and H2 settings, but ApplyJa3 installs the Chrome JA3, so the fingerprint is inconsistent.
- **Fix:** In _resolveUpstreamForTab, when an override exists, return `ja3: override.ja3 || null`. Do not fall back to this._activeJa3, because a stored null means template mode for that tab. If you ever need an explicit 'inherit global' mode, store a separate ja3Mode ('inherit'|'template'|'custom') in setTabUpstream and fall back only for 'inherit'.
- _Reported 2× (also: tabs-shell-main)_

#### A-191 🟡 TLS profile changes never align the User-Agent or client hints; the Electron build number leaks
- **Where:** `user-agent-utils.js:31` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The user picks 'Safari' in Settings / connect-direct. Requests carry a Safari ClientHello with a Chrome/132 UA and sec-ch-ua headers, which Safari never sends. Even with the Chrome template, the non-reduced UA version and missing Google Chrome brand identify Electron.
- **Fix:** Build one profile manifest shared by Go profiles.go and JS: per profile give the UA, whether it is Chromium, and the sec-ch-ua brands/platform. In applyOutboundUserAgentToMitmHeaders(headers, ordered, browser), do two things. (1) For a non-Chromium profile, replace User-Agent with the profile UA and delete every sec-ch-ua* header (both in headers and in orderedHeaders). (2) For a Chromium profile, reduce the UA with `s.replace(/Chrome\/(\d+)\.[\d.]+/, 'Chrome/$1.0.0.0')` and rewrite sec-ch-ua to include the matching brand ("Google Chrome"/"Microsoft Edge"). Also set the same UA on the tab session so navigator.userAgent matches the wire UA. Pass tabUpstream.browser from mitm-proxy.js:1428/1754.

#### A-196 ⚪ JS worker: httpInflight goes negative and inUse leaks, which wedges the legacy worker
- **Where:** `azure-tls-worker.js:562` · **Category:** race · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Under CUPNET_ALLOW_LEGACY_NODE_WORKER=1 (tests/test-dns-mitm.js): 4 in-flight requests on a key hit connection errors, a 5th is a waiter, and a DNS-rule change sends clear. The waiter is aborted and httpInflight becomes -1. The next clear never finishes, and the worker stops serving without exiting.
- **Fix:** In handleLine, add `let counted = false;` per attempt. Set `httpInflight++; counted = true;` after the borrow. Replace each decHttpInflight() with `if (counted) { counted = false; decHttpInflight(); }`. In borrowClient, wrap the create: `pool.inUse++; try { return { client: createAzureClient(profileName, proxy), key }; } catch (e) { pool.inUse--; const w = pool.waiters.shift(); if (w) { /* let waiter retry */ w(undefined); } throw e; }`, and treat an undefined resolve as a retry of the loop. Also make decHttpInflight notify when `httpInflight <= 0`, as a defensive measure.

#### A-197 ⚪ Legacy worker fresh and bare retries drop the custom JA3 and forceHttp1
- **Where:** `azure-tls-worker.js:503` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** With the legacy worker (tests / CUPNET_ALLOW_LEGACY_NODE_WORKER=1), a custom-JA3 profile on a flaky upstream silently switches to the template JA3 on retry, and youtube or DNS-override requests retry over HTTP/2 and fail again.
- **Fix:** In both Step A and Step B (in both the result.error branch and the catch branch), after creating the client add `if (ja3) { try { freshClient.applyJA3(ja3); } catch (_) {} }` (same for bareClient), and add `forceHttp1: forceHttp1 === true` to each retry request's options. Otherwise, delete the legacy worker.

#### A-198 ⚪ A non-string JSON id gets a reply with an empty id, so the caller hangs until its timeout
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/main.go:132` · **Category:** ipc-contract · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Any client (test script or future caller) that uses numeric ids never gets its response matched and waits the full timeout.
- **Fix:** In main.go, when unmarshalling idBytes into a string fails, reply with the raw id and skip the line: `if err := json.Unmarshal(idBytes, &id); err != nil { sendLine(map[string]any{"id": json.RawMessage(idBytes), "statusCode": 0, "error": "id must be a string"}); continue }`.

#### A-200 ⚪ Idle eviction uses the borrow time and the LRU cap stops at the first busy pool
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/pool.go:95` · **Category:** perf · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Long-polling tabs: after every long request the session pool is closed and re-handshaked, a connection churn real Chrome does not show. With one long-running tab at the LRU head, dozens of idle pools (each up to 4 sessions with connections) accumulate.
- **Fix:** In releaseSession, set `poolLastActivity[key] = time.Now()` under poolsMu (also on the waiter hand-off path). In evictPoolsIfNeeded, loop over poolLRU and skip busy or nil entries instead of breaking; stop when len(poolLRU) <= max or the scan is finished. Mirror both changes in azure-tls-worker.js.

#### A-201 ⚪ defaultUserAgentForBrowser has no prefix matching; JS and Go browser-name resolution differ
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/profiles.go:133` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A session profile with tlsProfile 'firefox_128': the Go session gets a Firefox ClientHello and H2 but a Chrome session UA, which is used by direct worker callers such as IP-geo that send no UA.
- **Fix:** In profiles.go add `func canonicalBrowser(b string) string` that trims, lowercases, and maps a prefix to a browserProfiles key (default "chrome"). Call it once at the top of createPooledSession and use the result for sessionBrowserName, defaultUserAgentForBrowser, http2FingerprintForBrowser and navigatorForJa3. For JS parity, lowercase `b` in azure-tls-worker.js http2FingerprintForBrowser (`const s = String(b).toLowerCase()`) before the startsWith checks.

#### A-202 ⚪ azuretls-go README docs describe a fallback to the Node FFI worker that no longer exists
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/README.md:27` · **Category:** compat · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** An operator sets CUPNET_USE_GO_WORKER=0 following the docs, and MITM refuses to start ('Go worker required; Node FFI worker disabled').
- **Fix:** Edit azuretls-go/README.md and azuretls-go/bin/README.md. State that the Go binary is required, that CUPNET_USE_GO_WORKER=0 and CUPNET_USE_NODE_WORKER=1 only take effect together with CUPNET_ALLOW_LEGACY_NODE_WORKER=1 (tests only), and remove the 'falls back to the Node worker' sentence.

#### A-282 ⚪ connect-direct does not reset forceHttp1 or the active fingerprint from the previous profile
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/mitm-tls-resilience-ipc.js:74` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** After using a profile with force_http1 and a custom UA, the user clicks 'Direct'. All traffic keeps using HTTP/1.1-only ALPN and the old profile's UA/timezone, and CDP logging re-applies that UA on attach.
- **Fix:** In connect-direct, after setUpstream(null): call `ctx.mitmProxy.setForceHttp1?.(false)`. Then, if ctx.activeFingerprint is set, loop over ctx.tabManager.getAllTabs() calling ctx.resetFingerprintOnWebContents(wc) and set `ctx.activeFingerprint = null`, the same way disconnectGlobalProxy does. Optionally, also pass directData's force_http1/UA through and apply them.
- _Reported 2× (also: mitm-intercept)_

#### A-290 ⚪ quick-connect-profile applies UA/timezone but not the profile's TLS profile, JA3 or forceHttp1
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/quick-connect-ipc.js:52` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Profile A (custom Chrome JA3, force_http1) is connected. The user quick-connects Profile B (firefox template, Firefox UA). Traffic now goes out with B's proxy and UA but A's custom JA3 and HTTP/1.1, a fingerprint mix that matches neither profile.
- **Fix:** Extract the TLS-apply block from global-proxy-connect.js:120-133 into a helper, applyProfileTls(ctx, row), that calls setBrowser, sets _activeJa3, calls setForceHttp1 and runs broadcastTlsProfileChanged. Call it from quick-connect-profile after applyFingerprintToAllTabs. Simpler: have quick-connect-profile delegate to connectGlobalProxyProfile(ctx, profileId), or remove the unused IPC channel and its preload binding.

#### A-330 ⚪ Worker timeouts misaligned: MITM timer equals the upstream timeout and ignores opts.timeout
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:553` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A slow upstream (25-30s) or one EOF followed by a slow fresh retry: the browser gets 'Worker request timeout' 502 even though Go succeeds moments later. Setting CUPNET_TIMEOUT_REQUEST_EDITOR_MS=60000 has no effect beyond 30s.
- **Fix:** In request(opts), compute `const toMs = Math.max(networkPolicy.timeouts.workerRequestMs, (Number(opts.timeout) || 0) * 3 + 5000);` and use it for setTimeout and the error message. Alternatively, give the Go retries one shared overall deadline (context.WithTimeout(in.Timeout)) that is shorter than the JS timer.

#### A-331 ⚪ clearSessions uses a fixed pending id, so concurrent calls clobber each other
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:586` · **Category:** race · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** setDnsOverrides and setBrowser fire back-to-back (e.g. at startup or on profile connect). One clear promise resolves false after 10s, and a later clear's result is lost because an old timer deleted its entry.
- **Fix:** Coalesce: `if (this._clearP) return this._clearP; this._clearP = new Promise(...).finally(() => { this._clearP = null; });`. Alternatively, have the timer check that the entry is still its own before deleting: `if (this.pending.get(id) === entry) this.pending.delete(id)`.

#### A-351 ⚪ The 'firefox' JA3 prefill preset is a Chrome/Firefox hybrid (Chrome cipher order plus ALPS 17613)
- **Where:** `proxy-manager-renderer.js:154` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A user switches a profile to custom JA3 and uses the Firefox prefill. Every request carries an impossible Firefox fingerprint with Chrome ciphers and ALPS, which is trivially flagged.
- **Fix:** Replace the firefox, safari and ios entries in TLS_JA3_PRESETS with JA3 strings captured from the azuretls v1.11 presets (e.g. hit tls.peet.ws/api/all through the Go worker with each browser template) or from real current browsers. Alternatively, drop the static table and prefill from the worker via a __get_profiles__ or JA3 query, and correct the 'measured' comment.

#### A-366 ⚪ afterPack hook throws on missing optional ffi deps or node-gyp failure and mutates the dev node_modules
- **Where:** `scripts/after-pack-azuretls-worker-native.cjs:109` · **Category:** build · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** `npm run dist:mac` on a machine where the ffi optional deps failed to install aborts with '[after-pack-azuretls] нет .../@2060.io/ref-napi'. After building the x64 DMG on arm64, the dev ffi addons are x64.
- **Fix:** At the top of the afterPack hook, return unless process.env.CUPNET_BUNDLE_LEGACY_WORKER === '1'. Replace the throws at lines 109, 122 and 127 with console.warn plus return. Alternatively, drop `"afterPack"` from the build config along with the `node scripts/ensure-cupnet-node.mjs &&` prefix in the dist* scripts, since the Go worker does not need bundled Node. If ensure-cupnet-node is kept, verify SHASUMS256.txt and take the arch from the target, not from process.arch.
- _Reported 2× (also: build-ci-tests)_

#### A-383 ⚪ test-fingerprints.js cannot work: it parses res.body (workers return bodyBase64) and never fails
- **Where:** `test-fingerprints.js:160` · **Category:** test-infra · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Running `node test-fingerprints.js` prints failures or null fingerprints but exits 0, so fingerprint regressions go unnoticed.
- **Fix:** Add a decode helper and use it at each call site: extractFingerprints(Buffer.from(res.bodyBase64 || '', 'base64').toString('utf8')). In assert(), set process.exitCode = 1 when a check fails. Change `return` to `continue` at line 56. Optionally spawn the Go worker binary (resolved like mitm-proxy.js _resolveGoWorkerBinary) instead of azure-tls-worker.js.

#### A-390 ⚪ UA injection pushes the stale Chrome/120 default into orderedHeaders even when headers has a real UA
- **Where:** `user-agent-utils.js:59` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — orderedHeaders mirrors the real headers UA.
- **Impact:** A script rule or caller supplies a UA only in the headers map. The wire UA becomes Chrome/120 macOS regardless of profile.
- **Fix:** In applyOutboundUserAgentToMitmHeaders, remember the sanitized headers UA (uaFromHeaders) in the first loop. At line 59 push ['User-Agent', uaFromHeaders || DEFAULT_CHROME_UA]. Separately, bump DEFAULT_CHROME_UA to a current Chrome major version (matching the Chromium 132 / Chrome 133 templates), or derive it from the active profile.

### Build / CI / tests

#### A-044 🟠 electron-builder 'files: **/*' packs real session dumps, backups and dev junk into app.asar
- **Where:** `package.json:112` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Run `npm run dist:mac` on this tree and publish the DMG. Anyone can run `npx asar extract` and get real HAR/session captures, the full git history bundle and the workspace backup.
- **Fix:** Replace build.files with an allowlist, e.g. ["main.js","main-process/**","preload*.js","*.html","*.css","*-renderer.js","<explicit list of required root *.js modules>","icons/**","data/**","azuretls/**","package.json","node_modules/**"] plus "!**/{tests,test-results,docs,_debug*,.ruru,.cursor,!MD}/**", "!**/*.{zip,bundle,har,log}", "!.env*". Add a post-build check that runs `npx asar list` and fails on forbidden patterns.
- _Reported 2× (also: azuretls-fingerprint)_

#### A-045 🟠 Mac x64 and arm64 DMGs share one artifactName, and the x64 app ships without an x64 Go worker or Node
- **Where:** `package.json:150` · **Category:** packaging · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Push tag v27.06.2026-6. The release gets a single MacOS_CupNet-27.6.2026-6.dmg, whichever arch finished last. If the x64 one wins, or an Intel user installs the x64 build, MITM never starts and every tab fails.
- **Fix:** Set mac.artifactName to 'MacOS_CupNet-${version}-${arch}.${ext}'. On the macOS CI job run `npm run build:go` (build.sh cross-compiles darwin amd64+arm64) instead of build:go:local. Make ensure-cupnet-node arch-aware, or drop the bundled Node since the legacy worker is disabled. In afterPack, assert that Resources/azuretls-go/bin/<name for context.arch> exists.

#### A-058 🟠 Six Vitest unit files import node:test, so `vitest run` fails and PR checks stay red
- **Where:** `tests/unit/session-cookie-utils.test.mjs:1` · **Category:** test-infra · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — swapped `node:test`→`vitest` import in the 6 unit files; `vitest run` now collects them (39 previously-dead tests, suite 32 files/179 tests green).
- **Impact:** pr-checks.yml `npm run test:unit:all` exits non-zero. Because it is `test:unit && test:go`, go test never runs, and the 'Integration tests' and 'E2E smoke' steps are skipped on every PR.
- **Fix:** In each of the six files, replace `import { describe, it } from 'node:test'` with `import { describe, it } from 'vitest'`. The node:assert/strict calls can stay, since Vitest reports thrown AssertionErrors as failures. Also check for any before/after/mock imports from node:test and map them to the Vitest equivalents (beforeAll/afterAll/vi).
- _Reported 2× (also: x-ipc-contract)_

#### A-159 🟡 createMitmAzureBackend requires a module that doesn't exist, so a plain-Node MitmProxy always throws
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:150` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Any tool or test that runs `new MitmProxy(...)` under node without the env flag (tests/test-mitm.js, ad-hoc scripts) crashes with 'Cannot find module ./azure-tls-inprocess'.
- **Fix:** In mitm-proxy.js, make createMitmAzureBackend(workerPath) simply `return new AzureTLSWorker(workerPath);`. Delete the in-process require/catch branch and its Russian hint text, and drop the CUPNET_AZURETLS_IN_PROCESS path from mitmUseChildAzureTlsWorker. Optionally remove the rebuild:azuretls* package.json scripts whose target files no longer exist.
- _Reported 4× (also: x-ipc-contract, mitm-intercept, azuretls-fingerprint)_

#### A-167 🟡 dist/pack scripts neither build nor verify the Go worker, so stale or missing binaries get packaged _(WIP)_
- **Where:** `package.json:47` · **Category:** build · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A developer runs `npm run dist:mac` or `./install.sh` after editing azuretls-go/*.go. The installed app runs the April worker without the watchdog or the fixes. On a clean checkout the app ships with no worker, and every MITM request fails with 'Go worker required'.
- **Fix:** Prefix dist/dist:*/pack with `npm run build:go &&`, or build:go:local plus an arch check. Change the Go extraResources filter to ["azuretls-worker-*", "!azuretls-worker-test"]. Add an afterPack assertion that the expected binary for context.arch exists, and optionally spawn it and check the __init__ handshake.

#### A-184 🟡 bisect-tab-nav.sh can permanently lose WIP files _(WIP)_
- **Where:** `scripts/bisect-tab-nav.sh:48` · **Category:** data-loss · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The user runs the bisect and presses Ctrl-C during the mitm-proxy.js probe. mitm-proxy.js is left at HEAD, and the WIP copy in /tmp is deleted by the trap.
- **Fix:** Keep the backups in a persistent dir (e.g. `BK="$ROOT/.bisect-backup"; mkdir -p "$BK"`) and replace the trap with a restore function: `restore(){ for b in "$BK"/*.wip; do [ -e "$b" ] || continue; f=$(basename "$b" .wip | tr '_' '/'); cp "$b" "$f"; done; rm -rf "$BK"; cleanup; }; trap restore EXIT INT TERM`. Better: encode paths reversibly instead of relying on the tr '_' mapping, since filenames can contain '_'. Write HEAD via a temp file: `git show "HEAD:$f" > "$f.head.tmp" && mv "$f.head.tmp" "$f"`. Add `TO=$(command -v timeout || command -v gtimeout)` and fail if it is empty. Simplest alternative: `git stash push -- "$f"` / `git stash pop` per file.

#### A-189 🟡 test-mitm.js forces exit 0, hiding 4 failing MitmProxy tests
- **Where:** `tests/test-mitm.js:263` · **Category:** test-infra · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** `npm test` in CI and in the nightly reports green while MitmProxy construction is broken in plain Node. A regression in the real buildHttpResponse would never be caught.
- **Fix:** Remove the trailing `setTimeout(() => process.exit(0), 500)`. Before `require('../mitm-proxy')`, set process.env.CUPNET_AZURETLS_CHILD_PROCESS='1' and CUPNET_ALLOW_LEGACY_NODE_WORKER='1', or stub the backend. Run it in run-all.sh as `node --test --test-force-exit tests/test-mitm.js`. Export buildHttpResponse (e.g. module.exports._testBuildHttpResponse) and test the real function with bodyBase64 input instead of the inline copy.

#### A-190 🟡 Go-worker NDJSON test helper drops lines, making the concurrency test hang
- **Where:** `tests/unit/azuretls-go-worker.test.mjs:47` · **Category:** test-infra · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — queued line reader; concurrency test passes 3/3.
- **Impact:** In 'concurrent requests return matching ids', 5 httpbin responses complete close together and two land in one pipe read. One line is lost, the 5th nextJson() never resolves, and the test hits the 15s testTimeout. This is the baseline 1 failure.
- **Fix:** In spawnWorker, replace nextJson with a queued reader: `const q=[], waiters=[]; rl.on('line', l => { const w = waiters.shift(); if (w) w(l); else q.push(l); }); const nextJson = () => new Promise((resolve, reject) => { const take = l => { try { resolve(JSON.parse(l)); } catch (e) { reject(e); } }; q.length ? take(q.shift()) : waiters.push(take); });`. Track the spawned procs and call proc.kill() in an afterEach.

#### A-194 ⚪ Release draft attaches executables from the unpacked build dirs (CupNet.exe, node.exe, the Go worker exe)
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/build-all.yml:154` · **Category:** packaging · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The v* tag release shows CupNet.exe (not runnable on its own without the unpacked directory), node.exe and the worker exe next to the installer. Users download the wrong file.
- **Fix:** In build-all.yml release-draft files, use top-level globs: `release-assets/*.dmg`, `release-assets/*.exe`, `release-assets/*.AppImage`, `release-assets/*.blockmap`. Also narrow the upload-artifact path to `dist/*.dmg dist/*.exe dist/*.AppImage dist/*.blockmap dist/latest*.yml`.

#### A-195 ⚪ Nightly reliability workflow never builds the Go worker
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/reliability-nightly.yml:20` · **Category:** test-infra · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A regression in the Go worker or its spawn path passes the nightly. A secret passed as a CUPNET_* env var (for example CUPNET_BEARER_TOKEN) ends up in a downloadable artifact.
- **Fix:** In reliability-nightly.yml add `- uses: actions/setup-go@v5` with go-version-file azuretls-go/go.mod, then `run: npm run build:go:local` before the tests and `run: npm run test:unit:all` after npm test. In scripts/stability-baseline.js collectEnv(), set `acc[k] = /TOKEN|KEY|PASS|SECRET|PROXY_URL/i.test(k) ? '[redacted]' : process.env[k]`.

#### A-199 ⚪ Windows parent watchdog treats any successful OpenProcess as alive
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/parent_watchdog_windows.go:7` · **Category:** leak · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** CupNet crashes on Windows and its PID is quickly reused. The Go worker keeps running as an orphan (relying only on stdin EOF).
- **Fix:** After OpenProcess succeeds: `defer windows.CloseHandle(h); var code uint32; if err := windows.GetExitCodeProcess(h, &code); err != nil { return false }; return code == 259 /* STILL_ACTIVE */`. To guard against PID reuse, optionally record the parent's creation time at startup with GetProcessTimes and compare it on each check. Run `go mod tidy` in azuretls-go so x/sys becomes a direct requirement.
- _Reported 2× (also: azuretls-fingerprint)_

#### A-244 ⚪ install.sh builds both mac arches but always installs the x64 app
- **Where:** `install.sh:9` · **Category:** packaging · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** On Apple Silicon, ./install.sh installs a Rosetta x64 app that looks for azuretls-worker-darwin-amd64 (stale or missing locally), so MITM breaks or runs old code.
- **Fix:** In install.sh, before the build add `npm run build:go`. Then set: `if [ "$(uname -m)" = "arm64" ]; then BUILD_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/dist/mac-arm64"; else BUILD_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/dist/mac"; fi`. Optionally build only the host arch with `npx electron-builder --mac dir --$( [ "$(uname -m)" = arm64 ] && echo arm64 || echo x64 )` to halve build time.

#### A-307 ⚪ Security WIP depends on untracked files, including data/common-passwords-10k.txt _(WIP)_
- **Where:** `main-process/services/password-policy.js:9` · **Category:** build · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The WIP is committed with `git commit -a` (tracked files only). CI's checkout lacks the new modules, so the app throws MODULE_NOT_FOUND on start, the unit tests fail, and release builds ship with no common-password list.
- **Fix:** Commit the untracked source, data and test files with the WIP: data/common-passwords-10k.txt, html-response-preview.js, preload-shell.js, profile-gate.html, profile-gate-renderer.js, main-process/ipc/ipc-trusted-sender.js, main-process/ipc/handlers/profile-ipc.js, main-process/services/{credentials-vault-crypto,password-policy,profile-avatars,profile-data-migration,profile-gate-window,profile-session,secure-fs,traffic-log-crypto,vault-session-service}.js, and tests/unit/*.test.mjs. Use `git add -A` after reviewing, not `git commit -a`. Add `azuretls-go/azuretls-go-worker` to .gitignore. In password-policy.js loadCommonPasswords, call sysLog('warn', ...) when the file is missing, or throw when app.isPackaged.

#### A-309 ⚪ Test and debug bypasses stay active in production builds
- **Where:** `main-process/services/profile-session.js:87` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** `CUPNET_SKIP_PROFILE_GATE=1 open -a CupNet` (or setting that env var for the user) opens the main browser window without the profile password prompt.
- **Fix:** Add a helper `const devOnlyEnv = (k) => !require('electron').app.isPackaged && process.env[k] === '1';` and use it for CUPNET_E2E, CUPNET_SKIP_PROFILE_GATE (profile-session.js:87, profile-data-migration.js:132, index.js:13, main-window.js:39, tab-open-confirm.js:18) and CUPNET_ALLOW_LEGACY_NODE_WORKER (mitm-proxy.js:376). Assign globalThis.__cupnetAppContext only under that E2E condition. Before enabling electronFuses (runAsNode:false, onlyLoadAppFromAsar:true, enableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation:true), switch the sub-windows.js:509 IVAC scout spawn to the bundled cupnet-node binary in extraResources instead of process.execPath + ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE. Enabling the fuses also requires upgrading electron-builder from ^24 to a version that supports electronFuses, or adding an @electron/fuses afterPack step.

#### A-332 ⚪ Packaged-app check `process.defaultApp === false` is never true
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:327` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** In an installed CupNet.app, running IVAC Scout (once the ReferenceError is fixed) fails with 'spawn node ENOENT', or with a module-not-found error for app.asar/scripts/debug-ivac.js.
- **Fix:** In sub-windows.js:508 and mitm-proxy.js:327 use `const isPackaged = !!(require('electron').app && require('electron').app.isPackaged) && !process.env.ELECTRON_IS_DEV;` (mitm-proxy may also run outside Electron, so guard the require). In sub-windows.js:515 replace `cwd: _cupnetRoot` with a writable directory, e.g. `cwd: isPackaged ? app.getPath('userData') : d.cupnetRoot`. Also add `scripts/debug-ivac.js` to build.asarUnpack and resolve it via `.replace('app.asar', 'app.asar.unpacked')` when packaged.

#### A-343 ⚪ npm scripts and the release workflow reference scripts that don't exist
- **Where:** `package.json:14` · **Category:** build · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Reproducing the release job locally, or any CI setup that installs azuretls deps, fails with 'Cannot find module scripts/azuretls-electron-rebuild.js'. Developers who follow the error hints hit the same error.
- **Fix:** Remove the rebuild:azuretls*, smoke:azuretls-native, check:native* and setup:ffi entries from package.json. Remove the 'AzureTLS native rebuild + smoke' step in build-all.yml:57-65. Change the hints in mitm-proxy.js:136-167 to point to `npm run build:go` (the FFI worker is disabled unless CUPNET_ALLOW_LEGACY_NODE_WORKER=1).
- _Reported 2× (also: azuretls-fingerprint)_

#### A-367 ⚪ ensure-cupnet-node downloads Node without verification and caches it without checking arch
- **Where:** `scripts/ensure-cupnet-node.mjs:55` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A developer once ran the dist scripts from an x64 terminal. Later arm64 packages keep shipping the x64 node from resources/cupnet-node.
- **Fix:** Write `${NODE_VER}-${key}` to VERSION and compare it against that (compute platformSpec() before the cache check). Download SHASUMS256.txt from the same dist directory and compare the sha256 of the archive before extracting. Or remove ensure-cupnet-node from the dist scripts, and resources/cupnet-node from extraResources, since the legacy worker is test-only.

#### A-368 ⚪ gen-register-main-ipc.cjs wipes register-main-ipc.js when run _(WIP)_
- **Where:** `scripts/gen-register-main-ipc.cjs:15` · **Category:** data-loss · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Someone runs `node scripts/gen-register-main-ipc.cjs` following its header comment. register-main-ipc.js becomes an empty function, the app starts with no IPC handlers, and the WIP changes to that file are gone.
- **Fix:** Delete scripts/gen-register-main-ipc.cjs. If it must stay, add near the top: `if (idxLines.length < 4645) { console.error('index.js no longer contains the IPC monolith; refusing to overwrite'); process.exit(1); }`, and require a `--force` flag before overwriting an existing OUT.

#### A-369 ⚪ test:mitm:live reports OK even when every proxied request fails
- **Where:** `scripts/mitm-standalone-check.mjs:131` · **Category:** test-infra · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** With no Go binary, or with a broken MITM TLS path, all requests return {ok:false}, yet the script prints '== DONE == OK' and exits 0.
- **Fix:** Push each fetchViaProxy result r into a `results` array, including the result after the restart. At the end, compute `const failed = restartErr || results.some(r => !r.ok || r.status < 200 || r.status >= 400);`, print 'FAILED' or 'OK' based on it, and call process.exit(failed ? 1 : 0). Optionally remove hardTimeout.unref(); the timer is already cleared at line 128. Also delete the unused `okStart`.

#### A-370 ⚪ rebuild-electron-native arch probe fails if Electron writes more than 64 bytes
- **Where:** `scripts/rebuild-electron-native.js:27` · **Category:** build · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Under an x64 (Rosetta) terminal on Apple Silicon, Electron prints a warning to stderr. The probe falls back to x64, and better-sqlite3 is rebuilt for x64 while Electron is arm64, so the app fails with 'incompatible architecture'.
- **Fix:** Change `maxBuffer: 64` to `maxBuffer: 1024 * 1024` and add `stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore']` so stderr is discarded, then keep `(r.stdout||'').trim()` as the result.

#### A-384 ⚪ E2E and PR workflow leak temp profiles and have no job timeout
- **Where:** `tests/e2e/helpers.js:41` · **Category:** leak · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Repeated local e2e runs fill /tmp with full Chromium profiles. A stuck MITM start on CI burns 6 hours of macOS minutes.
- **Fix:** helpers.js: keep userDataDir, return it with the app (e.g. `app.__userDataDir = userDataDir`), and in each spec's afterAll after `app.close()` call `fs.rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true })`. Alternatively, register `process.once('exit', () => fs.rmSync(userDataDir, {recursive:true, force:true}))` in launchCupnet. pr-checks.yml: add `timeout-minutes: 45` under `unit-and-integration:`. playwright.config.js: add `globalTimeout: 40 * 60_000`.

#### A-385 ⚪ run-all.sh word-splits the runner command, breaking on paths with spaces
- **Where:** `tests/run-all.sh:25` · **Category:** test-infra · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The repo is cloned to '/Users/x/My Projects/cupnet2' and `npm test` reports test-db.js as FAILED.
- **Fix:** Make run_test take the command as an argument array: `run_test() { local file="$1"; shift; local name="$(basename "$file")"; if [ $# -eq 0 ]; then set -- node; fi; ... if "$@" "$file"; then ...; }`. Call it as `run_test tests/test-db.js env ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 "$ELECTRON_BIN"`.

#### A-386 ⚪ test-db.js exits 0 ('skip') on native-arch mismatch, so DB regressions pass as green
- **Where:** `tests/test-db.js:38` · **Category:** test-infra · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** better-sqlite3 was rebuilt for a different arch than the Electron binary. `npm test` reports 13/13 passed without running any DB test.
- **Fix:** In tests/test-db.js change `process.exit(0)` to `process.exit(process.env.CI ? 1 : 77)`. In run-all.sh treat exit code 77 as SKIP with its own counter and print it in the results. Fix the header comment to say it uses a temp file DB.

#### A-387 ⚪ Unit tests give false confidence: ineffective vi.mock and tautological assertions
- **Where:** `tests/unit/cors-settings.test.mjs:6` · **Category:** test-infra · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A change in settings-store that calls app.getPath at import crashes in production, but the mocked test is expected to cover it and fails confusingly instead. The endpoint fallback test can never fail.
- **Fix:** (a) Remove vi.mock('electron'), or install a real shim before the require, for example `require.cache[require.resolve('electron')] = { exports: { app: { getPath: () => '/tmp/cupnet-vitest-userdata' } } }`, following the pattern in omnibox-history.test.mjs. (b) Drop vi.resetModules() and rely on _resetForTests(). Add a reset hook to profile-session.js if isolation matters, or delete the relevant require.cache entries in beforeEach. (c) Replace the tautology with an assertion on the actual expected behaviour, for example `assert.deepStrictEqual(r.endpoints.map(e => e.path ?? e), [])`, or whatever list the fallback is meant to produce.

### Tabs & shell

#### A-002 🔴 Cookie IPC handlers remove-cookie / clear-cookies / consolidate-cookies have no sender check; get-cookies/set-cookie ownership check allows full-jar access
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/cookies-dns-ipc.js:86` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Any tab renderer that calls clearCookies with another tab's id wipes that tab's cookie group. getCookies with no tabId, called from the active tab, returns HttpOnly cookies of unrelated sites in the same partition.
- **Fix:** (1) In _tabForCookieAccess, return the owned tab only when the sender is an internal page: `const u = event.senderFrame?.url || event.sender.getURL(); if (!u.startsWith('file://')) return null;`. Everything else then falls through to assertTrustedShell. (2) In remove-cookie, clear-cookies and consolidate-cookies, replace `const tab = tabId ? ctx.tabManager.getTab(tabId) : ctx.tabManager.getActiveTab();` with `const tab = _cookieSessionTab(event, tabId);` and take `event` as the first parameter. (3) Optionally drop getCookies/clearCookies from preload-view.js for non-file:// origins, or strip httpOnly cookies from results returned to tab senders.

#### A-034 🟠 apply-launch-profile-from-request passes an un-normalized profile to loadSessionProfile and always crashes after side effects
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/session-profile-ipc.js:178` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — apply-launch-profile normalizes via parseLaunchProfile.
- **Impact:** In Log Viewer, choose 'Apply launch profile' on a request. The proxy is switched and an empty tab is opened, then the call fails with 'Cannot read properties of undefined (reading recording)'.
- **Fix:** In the apply-launch-profile-from-request handler, add const parsed = parseLaunchProfile(built.profile); if (!parsed.ok) return { success:false, error: parsed.error }; return loadSessionProfile(ctx, parsed.profile, loadOpts);. parseLaunchProfile is already imported at the top of the file.

#### A-035 🟠 Closing a tab can strip X-CupNet-Rid for the whole shared session (interceptor ref-count imbalance)
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/tabs-ipc.js:43` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Start the app (tab1 attached, count=1), click '+' (tab2 not attached), then close tab2. The count drops to 0, onBeforeSendHeaders(null) runs on persist:cg_1, and tab1's requests reach MITM without X-CupNet-Rid. Per-tab upstream lookup and MITM/CDP log dedup stop working for tab1.
- **Fix:** Remove the `tabSession.webRequest.onBeforeSendHeaders(null)` and `onHeadersReceived(null)` calls from _removeAllHooks in request-interceptor.js, since tab-manager owns those listeners and the interceptor no longer registers webRequest hooks. Also move attach/detach into tabManager.createTab/setTabCookieGroup/closeTab (or call attachToSession in the new-tab and open-settings-tab handlers) so the ref count stays balanced.

#### A-041 🟠 Reload/F5 navigates to a stale URL instead of reloading the current page
- **Where:** `main-process/services/tab-error-page.js:162` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Click a link on site A (the retry entry becomes that link), then type site B in the address bar and press Reload. The tab goes back to site A's link. Clicking a logout or add-to-cart GET link that redirects, then pressing Reload, repeats that action.
- **Fix:** In reloadActiveTab, change the condition to `if (isTabShowingErrorPage(tab)) return reloadFailedTabNavigation(tab);` and otherwise call wc.reload() or wc.reloadIgnoringCache(). In tab-manager.js did-navigate (non-error URL branch, around line 511), also set `tab.pendingNavigationRetry = null` (or do it inside clearTabErrorPageState, but not from the will-navigate call site). Either capture the real method/body for form POSTs or skip the retry when the method is unknown, so a POST is not replayed as a GET.

#### A-054 🟠 WebRTC leak protection is ineffective and is not applied to per-tab-proxy or recreated tabs
- **Where:** `tab-manager.js:410` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** With a proxy connected, a page's WebRTC STUN candidates reveal the user's real public IP. Tabs that use a per-tab proxy with no global upstream, and tabs that were isolated, run with the 'default' policy.
- **Fix:** Change applyWebRtcPolicy to `webContents.setWebRTCIPHandlingPolicy('disable_non_proxied_udp')` and drop the no-op port range call. Add a helper, e.g. applyWebRtcPolicyForTab(tab), that applies the policy when `_upstreamProxyRules || tab.proxyProfileId` and resets it otherwise. Call it in createTab, in setTabCookieGroup after the new view is created, in setTabProxy (or the set-tab-proxy handler), and in setProxyAll, using that per-tab condition instead of the global flag alone.
- _Reported 2× (also: proxy)_

#### A-056 🟠 HTTP >= 400 heuristic replaces real server pages (e.g. Cloudflare 403 challenge) with the internal error page, and the result is racy
- **Where:** `tab-manager.js:584` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Open a Cloudflare-protected site that returns a 403 challenge. Sometimes the tab shows 'Page not available' instead of the Turnstile widget, and clicking Reload shows the error page again.
- **Fix:** Remove the HTTP-status swap from did-finish-load. Keep the internal error page for did-fail-load network errors only. If an HTTP-error page is still wanted, put it behind an opt-in setting and only use it for empty bodies, and never for responses that carry cf-mitigated/cf-ray/server: cloudflare. Separately, stop registering a per-tab onCompleted on shared sessions: register it once per session and dispatch by webContentsId, and reset _statusTrackerAttached when the session changes.

#### A-099 🟡 Re-applying per-tab proxy after isolate / cookie-group change re-rolls {SID}/{RAND}, changing the exit IP
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/cookies-dns-ipc.js:336` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A logged-in tab on a sticky residential proxy is isolated; its exit IP changes and the site invalidates the session. An exported launch profile reproduces a different IP.
- **Fix:** In set-tab-proxy, store the resolved upstream config on the tab (for example tab.mitmUpstream = {upstream, browser, ja3, forceHttp1} or tab.proxyResolvedVars captured through parseProxyTemplate's resolvedOut). In isolate-tab and set-tab-cookie-group, do not call setTabUpstream again; only re-apply the fingerprint to the new webContents (ctx.applyFingerprintFromProfile). In export-launch-profile, when the request's tab has a proxyProfileId, use that profile and the tab's stored vars. Otherwise resolve with ctx.connectedResolvedVars instead of {}.

#### A-122 🟡 Anti-anti-debug eval hook turns every direct eval into global eval
- **Where:** `main-process/services/anti-anti-debug-script.js:187` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** On a devtools-hostile site, scripts that use direct eval over local variables throw ReferenceError and the page breaks.
- **Fix:** Remove the eval hook block (anti-anti-debug-script.js:178-192) and rely on the Function-constructor and setTimeout/setInterval string hooks. Direct-eval semantics cannot be kept from a JS wrapper. If debugger stripping in eval is still needed, do it on the MITM path by rewriting the script source.

#### A-129 🟡 Menu Cmd+W / Cmd+R act on the browser tab even when a tool window is focused
- **Where:** `main-process/services/main-window.js:285` · **Category:** ux-broken · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Pressing Cmd+W in Log Viewer or Notes closes the active browser tab, losing its page state, instead of closing the tool window.
- **Fix:** Change the handlers to click: (item, focusedWindow) => { if (focusedWindow && d.mainWindow && focusedWindow !== d.mainWindow) { focusedWindow.close(); return; } ...existing... }. For Reload and Force Reload, use focusedWindow.webContents.reload() or reloadIgnoringCache() when a non-main window is focused, or just return.

#### A-130 🟡 open-in-new-tab ignores opener cookie group and per-tab proxy, and accepts file:// URLs
- **Where:** `main-process/services/main-window.js:210` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** In an isolated tab using proxy profile X, a target=_blank link opens in the Default group through the global proxy, which exposes a different identity and IP.
- **Fix:** In the open-in-new-tab branch, pass cookieGroupId: opener?.cookieGroupId ?? 1 and proxyProfileId: opener?.proxyProfileId ?? null to createTab. After creating the tab, copy the opener's MITM upstream to the new tab (for example mitmProxy.setTabUpstream(newTabId, <opener's stored upstream config>)) and apply the profile fingerprint. That means exposing the applyMitmAndFingerprintForTabProxy logic, or a stored per-tab upstream, through ctx. Optionally reject resolved URLs that are not http(s) or about:blank when they come from setWindowOpenHandler.
- _Reported 2× (also: browser-ui)_

#### A-146 🟡 Launch profile: tab.isolated ignored; clearCookies/clearStorage wipe the shared Default partition _(WIP)_
- **Where:** `main-process/services/session-profile-loader.js:38` · **Category:** data-loss · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — isolated/clear profiles get a dedicated cookie group.
- **Impact:** Loading a profile marked isolated with clearStorageBeforeLoad erases localStorage/IndexedDB/cookies for every site in the user's Default group.
- **Fix:** In session-profile-loader.js around line 38: `let cookieGroupId = profile.tab.cookieGroupId || null; if (!cookieGroupId && (profile.tab.isolated || profile.clearCookiesBeforeLoad || profile.clearStorageBeforeLoad)) { const g = await ctx.db.createCookieGroupAsync(profile.tab.cookieGroupName || `Session ${Date.now()}`); if (!g) return { success:false, error:'Failed to create cookie group' }; cookieGroupId = g.id; } cookieGroupId = cookieGroupId || 1;`. Also refuse the clear* flags when newTab=false and the active tab is in group 1, unless the user explicitly asked for that.

#### A-149 🟡 onCompleted main-frame status tracker breaks for all but the newest tab on a shared session, and is never re-attached after a cookie-group change
- **Where:** `main-process/services/tab-error-page.js:176` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Open two tabs in the Default group. The first tab never shows the HTTP error page or records lastMainFrameUrl. Isolating a tab disables status tracking for it permanently.
- **Fix:** Keep a WeakSet of sessions that already have a tracker and register one onCompleted per session. Its callback looks up the tab with getTabIdByWebContentsId(details.webContentsId) (or a callback passed in from tab-manager) and sets lastMainFrameStatus/lastMainFrameUrl on that tab. Remove the per-tab _statusTrackerAttached flag, or reset it in setTabCookieGroup before calling attachTabListeners.

#### A-188 🟡 Session permission handler auto-grants every permission (mic, geolocation, notifications, openExternal)
- **Where:** `tab-manager.js:712` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A page requesting geolocation or microphone access is granted immediately with no user interaction.
- **Fix:** In installMediaPermissionHandlers, always install handlers and never pass null. Use an allowlist such as {'clipboard-sanitized-write','fullscreen','pointerLock'}. Deny, or route through a shell prompt, 'geolocation', 'notifications', 'openExternal', 'midi*', 'hid', 'serial' and 'usb'. For 'media', deny video when cameraMode==='none' and otherwise prompt or deny by default. Make the check handler mirror the request handler, returning false or 'prompt'-equivalent for denied types, so navigator.permissions reports 'prompt'/'denied' instead of 'granted'.

#### A-245 ⚪ Keyboard shortcut reference is out of sync with real bindings
- **Where:** `keyboard-shortcuts.js:10` · **Category:** ux-broken · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A user following the table opens Network Activity when trying to open Credentials.
- **Fix:** Update keyboard-shortcuts.js. Set logViewer keys to 'CmdOrCtrl+Shift+L' (label 'Open Network Activity / Log Viewer'), credentials to 'CmdOrCtrl+Alt+L' and proxyManager to 'CmdOrCtrl+P'. Remove the findInPage row or scope it to 'Tool windows'. Either note that Settings works from the toolbar only, or add a menu item with accelerator 'CmdOrCtrl+,' that calls openSettingsTab. Optionally add a unit test that checks these entries against the menu template accelerators.

#### A-311 ⚪ Post-load tasks fire on subframe failures / aborts; services attached only after navigation starts
- **Where:** `main-process/services/session-profile-loader.js:238` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** An ad iframe that fails to load triggers runAfterLoad early. The initial document request is not logged or intercepted.
- **Fix:** Change onFail to `(_, code, desc, _url, isMainFrame) => { if (!isMainFrame || code === -3) return; log; cleanup without running tasks (set done=true, clear timer, remove listeners) }`. Because once() consumes the listener, register it with `wc.on` and remove it in finish. Also move `await _attachTabServices(ctx, tab)` so it runs before `ctx.tabManager.navigate(...)` at line 118, and make setupNetworkLogging awaited inside _attachTabServices rather than void.

#### A-318 ⚪ isTabErrorPageUrl matches any URL containing 'error-page.html'
- **Where:** `main-process/services/tab-error-page.js:8` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Visiting any site path or query containing 'error-page.html' mislabels the tab.
- **Fix:** const ERROR_PAGE_HREF = pathToFileURL(ERROR_PAGE_PATH).href; function isTabErrorPageUrl(url) { const u = String(url || ''); if (!u.startsWith('file:')) return false; return u.split(/[?#]/)[0] === ERROR_PAGE_HREF; } Alternatively, compare decodeURIComponent of both if paths may be encoded differently.

#### A-371 ⚪ Imported/exported cookies lose host-only scope; __Host- cookies always fail
- **Where:** `session-profile.js:240` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A session relying on __Host-session cannot be reproduced from an exported launch profile.
- **Fix:** In export-launch-profile.js, add `hostOnly: !!c.hostOnly` and `sameSite: c.sameSite` to each exported cookie. In session-profile.js _normalizeCookies, compute `const hostOnly = row.hostOnly === true || name.startsWith('__Host-') || (row.hostOnly == null && !domain.startsWith('.'))` (or use the last clause only when an explicit hostOnly flag is absent). Omit `domain` from the output when hostOnly is true. For __Host- cookies, force `path: '/'` and `secure: true`.

#### A-381 ⚪ _loadTabUrl reports aborted/duplicate load errors to the shell
- **Where:** `tab-manager.js:76` · **Category:** error-handling · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Type URL A, then immediately B: an error banner appears for A. A failed DNS lookup produces two error events.
- **Fix:** In the _loadTabUrl catch: `const code = Number(err?.errno ?? err?.code); if (code === -3 || /ERR_ABORTED/.test(String(err?.message))) return;` Also skip the emit when the rejection matches a did-fail-load already reported, since did-fail-load already covers network errors. The simplest option is to emit only when the message does not match /\(-\d+\) loading/ (argument/URL errors).

#### A-382 ⚪ Address bar does nothing after the last tab is closed
- **Where:** `tab-manager.js:1062` · **Category:** ux-broken · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Close the only tab, type a URL, press Enter: nothing happens.
- **Fix:** In the navigate-to handler (tabs-ipc.js), when ctx.tabManager.getActiveTabId() is null, create a tab first (`const id = await ctx.tabManager.createTab({ url, cookieGroupId: 1 }); ctx.tabManager.switchTab(id);`, attaching the interceptor the same way the new-tab path does) and return. Alternatively, have closeTab/close-tab open a new-tab page when the last tab is closed.

### X-cut: lifecycle/leaks

#### A-057 🟠 X-CupNet-Rid handler is per-session but bound to the last created tab id, so per-tab proxy and log attribution go to the wrong tab
- **Where:** `tab-manager.js:1385` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Open tab A in cookie group 1 and give it a per-tab proxy profile (cookies-dns-ipc setTabUpstream(A)). Open tab B in group 1. Tab A's requests are now tagged with B's id, and MITM routes them through the global upstream, or DIRECT, so tab A leaks the real IP. Tab B's traffic can go through A's proxy the other way round. Network logs also attribute requests to the wrong tab. After B closes, the tag keeps pointing at the closed tab id.
- **Fix:** In applyCupNetRidHeaderToSession, drop the captured tabId. Inside the handler, compute `const tid = (details.webContentsId != null && getTabIdByWebContentsId(details.webContentsId)) || 'unknown';` and build the header from that. Guard with a WeakSet of sessions so the handler is installed once per session. Stop request-interceptor._removeAllHooks from nulling onBeforeSendHeaders, or re-apply the RID handler afterwards.

#### A-060 🟡 Go worker: __clear_sessions__ blocks the stdin reader until all in-flight requests finish, and waitIfClearing has a lost-wakeup race
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/main.go:137` · **Category:** race · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A tab has a 30s long-poll open and the user edits a DNS rule. For up to 30s the proxy stops processing requests entirely, and JS-side 30s timeouts turn them into 502s. Occasionally a request goroutine hangs forever after a clear (goroutine leak), and the browser gets a 502 on worker timeout.
- **Fix:** Replace the clearing, clearWaiters and httpInflight machinery with `var clearMu sync.RWMutex`. Each request goroutine does `clearMu.RLock(); defer clearMu.RUnlock()` for its whole lifetime. Move the clear into its own goroutine so the stdin loop keeps reading: `go func(){ clearMu.Lock(); closeAllPoolClients(); clearMu.Unlock(); sendLine(ok) }()`. That removes the lost wakeup and the Add(1) gap. To also avoid waiting for long-polls, skip the drain: under poolsMu, swap `pools` for a fresh map and close only the idle sessions of the old pools. releaseSession already closes sessions whose pool entry is missing (pool.go:204-209).
- _Reported 3× (also: azuretls-fingerprint, mitm-intercept)_

#### A-131 🟡 No render-process-gone handling for the shell or tab renderers
- **Where:** `main-process/services/main-window.js:80` · **Category:** error-handling · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The browser.html shell renderer crashes (OOM, GPU crash): the main window goes blank with no toolbar and cannot be recovered without restarting the app. A tab renderer crash leaves a blank tab with a stale title, no error page and no reload.
- **Fix:** In createMainWindow, add `d.mainWindow.webContents.on('render-process-gone', (_e, details) => { log; if (details.reason !== 'clean-exit' && !d.mainWindow.isDestroyed()) d.mainWindow.loadFile(d.getAssetPath('browser.html')); })`. Move the did-finish-load init into a named function that runs on every did-finish-load. In tab-manager.js attachTabListeners, add `wc.on('render-process-gone', (_e, details) => { if (details.reason === 'clean-exit') return; loadTabErrorPage(wc, tab, { code: details.reason, url: tab.url, ... }) })`, or clear the stale title and broadcast the tab list with a crashed flag so the user can reload.

#### A-136 🟡 Profile switch keeps the previous profile's tabs, proxy, logging session and cookies, and never re-applies profile-scoped MITM settings
- **Where:** `main-process/services/profile-gate-window.js:34` · **Category:** correctness · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Profile A is connected to proxy P with tabs open. The user switches to profile B. B sees A's tabs and cookies, its traffic still goes through A's proxy, and it is logged into A's DB session. B's chosen TLS profile and CORS setting are ignored until MITM restarts. While the gate is shown, hidden tabs keep generating traffic that is logged with redaction because the vault is locked.
